# READ THIS FIRST EVERY SESSION
Always read this file first, then check `/Users/cyberpwn/development/workspace/AuramMods/Witchery/PORTING.md`, then `/Users/cyberpwn/development/workspace/AuramMods/Witchery/PORTING_MANIFEST.md` before making porting changes.
Validation workflow for this project should use `./gradlew compileJava` (do not rely on `runClient` during routine scaffolding checks).

## Held Item Models and Legacy Sound Inventory (2026-11-23)

- The Witch's Hand uses the original hand texture and cuboids with their first-person and third-person finger poses. Invisibility gives the local first-person hand 20% opacity and hides it in third person. Death's Hand still deploys its scythe through the ordinary use input. The Brew Bag's original bottle renders translucent glass in the saved dye color and keeps the cork untinted. Both restored items keep their original sprites in inventory, on the ground, and in frames.
- The packaged Forge client and dedicated server showed the new hand poses, invisibility, two Brew Bag colors, the ordinary Brew Bag menu, and Death's Hand deployment. The model audit resolved all 441 default item models and 1,988 visible block states. The client loaded the restored `mob.darkmark.idle` clip and received its server sound packet. Dark Marks retain their usual Torment laugh.
- The JAR retains all 687 original PNGs, ten texture animation metadata files, and 88 original Ogg clips byte-for-byte. Five PNGs use updated paths. The reference audit resolved 2,297 local model and texture references and 89 sample references over 63 sound events. The original `random.loved` definition ships without a clip. Torment's original death definition also points to an absent clip, and the port retains its pitched hurt sample for the death event.
- Java 17 data generation, reobfuscation, and all 163 Forge GameTests passed. Both sides ran SHA-256 `15811997e9e1c08a497cc4b25c4e19f73f71a99cd4c1ba64949c3349e38cae78`. The background client used zero master volume and OpenAL No Output and did not take focus. The paired captures and logs are in `build/qa-legacy-assets`. Earmuffs still use a leather helmet when worn and need their original model.

## Registry Names, Subtitles, and JEI Localization (2026-11-23)

- English, German, Spanish, Russian, and Simplified Chinese now share 1,414 language keys. This pass fills the remaining 246 keys that previously fell back to English and adds 51 keys that the registries requested even in English. Eighteen of the latter are original spawn eggs in the creative tab. Registered compatibility eggs have readable names. The older functional broom item displays as an Enchanted Broom.
- The client resolved the names of 122 blocks and 56 entities and the default tooltips for 441 registered items and 436 Witchery creative stacks. Held samples included a colored Shaded Glass block, a bound Taglock Kit, armor, a grenade, and original and older egg IDs. The client resolved and formatted all 1,414 keys and loaded all 46 configured sound subtitles in each language.
- JEI 15.20.0.129 exposed all five machines and 102 recipe layouts. Every recipe note fit its category after client font wrapping in each language. The oven displayed its actual 30% fume chance with one percent sign, and the client opened the Kettle recipe screen in German and Simplified Chinese.
- Local Java 17 data generation, reobfuscation, and all 163 Forge GameTests passed. The standalone Forge client and dedicated server with JEI used SHA-256 `9857fd4d9e8fd0142308f438e358fd72b772fa7810fbaddb1285604343dae3b5`. The client stayed hidden with zero master volume and OpenAL No Output, and its process never took focus. The logs and recipe captures are in `build/qa-registry-locales`.

## Sun and Duplicate Grenade Fidelity (2026-11-23)

- A Sun Grenade owns one temporary light at a time and saves its last position under the original `BlockPlaced` keys. Movement and a burst release that position immediately. If another Sun Grenade shares the position, it keeps the light. A scheduled recheck also clears temporary lights left by external removal or older port saves. Permanent light blocks are preserved.
- The original undead check includes players with an active Infused Brew of the Grave. The packaged survival client used the actual throw and drink inputs. A controlled landed Sun Grenade recorded 59 fire ticks on the player and 99 after the burst. The preceding build recorded no fire in either case. The moving-light fixture counted one lit cell on the new build and two on the preceding build.
- The Sun and Duplicate projectiles use the original 0.3-block camera-facing quads and separate Quartz Sphere and variant textures. In matching views, both rendered sprites measured 26 pixels across. The preceding build measured 44 pixels and rendered item glint. The original carried items retain their glint.
- Java 17 JAR reobfuscation and all 163 checked-in GameTests passed. The matching clean Forge client and dedicated server used SHA-256 `488c92924d4d8d9950cc3f65b03f68414f984f0bd70ecc0ddc4df928aef0064f`. The normal dedicated restart restored the positioned grenade and its light. A controlled fuse expiry cleared that light and returned one Quartz Sphere. Every client run used zero master volume and No Output with no focus activation. Local evidence is in `build/qa-sun-grenade`.

## Legacy Player Mirror State and Wolf Cooldowns (2026-11-23)

- The original `WitcheryExtendedPlayer` stores the Mirror room coordinates and two escape timestamps. The importer relocates the active room and timers to `ForgeData.PlayerPersisted`, including custom legacy Mirror dimension IDs. The room only imports for a player inside Mirror. Existing modern entries take precedence. A successful escape records its cooldown even when the player has no prior persistent state.
- `LastHowl` and `LastBoneFind` retain the original elapsed one-minute cooldowns in epoch milliseconds. Earlier 1.20.1 saves still honor their active game-tick cooldowns through their next use. The original `PotionThrowing` counter is retained as dormant save data.
- Java 17 JAR reobfuscation and all 162 checked-in Forge GameTests passed. The player import tests cover a custom Mirror dimension ID, both cooldown clocks, an earlier 1.20.1 record, and a player who has already left Mirror.
- Clean Forge ran the same JAR on client and server, SHA-256 `3be9d2d5f862097f921c07598daefcb5c81163b75afb441c6b591d503c3c74f3`. A synthetic 1.7.10 survival record with a saved Moon Charm joined a disposable Mirror dimension. The client sent both chat phrases and the crouch and use howl input. Both phrases and the early howl were denied during cooldown. A later howl paralyzed the test villager, and the home phrase then reached the saved coordinates. The save retained the original throwing skill and bone-finding timestamp. The client used No Output and zero master volume in a hidden window with no focus activation.
- The same client rejoined after a normal `--nogui` restart. The room and both cooldowns survived, including the timer refreshed by the successful home chant. The original `I give up` chant reached the Overworld once its remaining old timer elapsed, cleared the room, and saved the new escape tick. Both sessions finished without audio or focus activation. Local evidence is in `build/qa-legacy-player/verified.log` and `build/qa-legacy-player/restart.log`.

## Morph and Resizing Compatibility (2026-11-23)

- Legacy Morph took ownership of the first-person renderer. The packaged Forge baseline with Woodwalkers 5.8.13 and CraftedCore 5.8.3 showed that the player's Resizing effect doubled the Pig collision bounds, while Woodwalkers read an unscaled model and eye height from its shape.
- The optional bridge reads the active shape through Woodwalkers' API. It applies the existing Resizing scale to that shape, resets the previous shape, and refreshes the player's collision after a transformation resets its bounds. Woodwalkers still controls its rendering and camera. Its separate eye-height query for a crouching humanoid also uses the shape's scale.
- The muted packaged client verified twice-normal Pig and Cat forms and restored the 3.6-block player collision height on returning to human form. The crouching Zombie's eye height rose from 1.364 to 2.728 blocks. Shrinking the Pig to 40% set a 0.36-block collision height and a 0.306-block eye height. The active shape followed the master integration setting when it was turned off and back on.
- Java 17 JAR reobfuscation and all 161 checked-in Forge GameTests passed. A clean headless Forge server ran the exact JAR without Woodwalkers and verified growth and effect removal on an ordinary Pig.
- After a dedicated restart the survival client rejoined as the enlarged Pig with a 1.8-block hitbox and 1.53-block eye height. Clearing Resizing kept the Pig shape and returned the actual server hitbox and client measurements to normal. The isolated client used No Output, zero master volume, and no focus activation. The tested JAR SHA-256 is `c0a8264dbde24e5d9b92f3eb5e3c813c4432a8ba8ff9009b4bec4d1e56ae5c1c`. See `baseline-retry.log`, `verified.log`, `clean.log`, and `restart-complete.log` in the directory named by `/tmp/witchery-woodwalkers-current`.

## Optional Mod Configuration (2026-11-23)

- `allowModIntegration` in `config/witchery-common.toml` controls optional integrations on each Forge installation. `allowModJEI` in `config/witchery-client.toml` independently controls the client's Witchery JEI tabs, crafting extensions, and subtypes. Both default to true. Restart the game or dedicated server after changing its config file.
- `witchery:mod_integration` contributes the Industrial Foregoing capture, duplication, and Crusher tags plus Thaumaturge's Witchery aspect maps. Its ID stays in the pack list in both modes and its namespaces load only when the setting is enabled.
- Java 17 data generation, reobfuscation, and all 161 checked-in Forge GameTests passed locally. The new test covers the three machine tags.
- The same packaged headless world loaded with integration enabled, disabled, and re-enabled. Witchery's Industrial Foregoing tags and Thaumaturge aspects followed the switch. Disabling it made the Sower use its default Rowan placement and left Thaumaturge's own aspect calculation and the Witchery Antidote recipe available. Re-enabling restored the original overrides.
- A packaged Jade and JEI client presented all five machine categories with both switches on. Turning off just JEI retained the Jade dirt disguise and vanilla crafting categories. Turning off the master switch retained vanilla crafting and showed Pit Dirt. Re-enabling restored the five categories and cover tooltip after the server reloaded. Both client sessions used zero master volume, the No Output device, and a hidden window with no recorded focus activation by the client.
- The Forge server also reopened a copy of the preceding 1.20.1 workshop save. The new pack was enabled automatically and the saved Brazier cook time and Crystal Ball cooldown were retained. The tested JAR SHA-256 is `d0307ad36a3bbf98dab200962df0c25265efd7dd321a6801368ab1590ed1f042`. See `headless.log`, `headless-resume.log`, and `client-matrix/switches.log` in the directory named by `/tmp/witchery-integration-config-current`.

## Arthana Range for Powered Gameplay (2026-11-23)

- Original `PowerSources.get` filters candidates by the distance from each core and that altar's range. Its radius argument is unused. The inclusive base range of 16 blocks extends to 32 when an Arthana is mounted on an altar member.
- The same lookup now serves the Brazier, Crystal Ball readings and scrying, powered rite activation, Infusion Recharging upkeep, protection wards, and the Hell on Earth destination. The Brazier and active wards recheck their cached source when the range changes.
- All 160 checked-in Forge GameTests passed. The new checks cover the base and extended ranges, a live Graveyard Mist batch, the Crystal Ball's 500-power charge and cooldown, and the Rite of Infusion Recharging. The focused barrier fixture now verifies activation and upkeep from an upgraded altar beyond 16 blocks.
- The packaged Forge survival client lit a Graveyard Mist batch 31 blocks from the core and mounted and remounted the Arthana by hand. The server fixture supplied the inputs and removed the blade to confirm the batch paused at the same cook time. A Crystal Ball at 32 blocks drew power, saved its cooldown, and opened a Bound Waystone view. A ball 33 blocks away remained idle. Restoring a saved Hell on Earth wave at its first summoning pulse produced a Magma Cube 32 blocks from the altar.
- The dedicated restart kept the mounted range and Crystal Ball cooldown. The Brazier resumed at 1,198 ticks from the 1,197 recorded on disk. Removing the blade after restart terminated a restored Protection ward outside the base range and paused the Brazier at 1,221 ticks.
- The tested JAR SHA-256 is `6ea906b41f2cc78cf5db4203ed5ab0a82b27e2a933c7a7fbcac79d600c865c2e`. The isolated client used zero master volume and the No Output device. The focus monitor recorded no activation. See `verified.log` and `restart-verified.log` in the directory named by `/tmp/witchery-altar-reach-current`. The subsequent standalone local `jar` build passed and differed only in the manifest build timestamp.

## Mounted Player Save Import (2026-11-23)

- Minecraft 1.7.10 saved a rider's vehicle in the player's `Riding` compound. Modern login reads `RootVehicle` and uses its `Attach` UUID to find the player's immediate mount. The importer converts the old vehicle tree with the same entity fixes used for chunk entities and passes the new record to the dedicated and integrated login paths.
- The disposable fixture moved the original Forge broom named Legacy Sky Rider from its chunk into a synthetic 1.7.10 player record. It used UUID `ad44f2df-90bb-4811-846b-12c2ee4ef44e` and brush color 6. The preceding packaged build joined at the old position on foot, and the named broom was absent from the loaded world.
- The muted single-player client loaded an original world copy with that record in `level.dat`. It started mounted and Save and Quit retained the broom, its attachment UUID and its dye. The fixture protected the test rider from an archived Huntsman while leaving the original mount intact.
- The nested-mount GameTest exposed the vanilla fixer carrying the inner rider's remaining fields into the outer vehicle. The importer now fixes each entity before rebuilding the passenger links. On dedicated Forge, a synthetic rider record put the original broom on a separately identified vanilla boat. The client resumed on the original broom with the right dye, and the server kept the outer boat and exactly one copy of the broom.
- An ordinary dedicated restart kept the same rider attachment, broom UUID, brush color and boat UUID. The matching packaged single-player and dedicated clients were muted and did not take desktop focus. All four focused import GameTests and all 158 required GameTests passed on the rebuilt code.
- The reobfuscated JAR SHA-256 was `e8d86594f1e7c97f20b75064f7fa0b60f3263582d64d02eb50a8418cfb92747e`. The fixture and logs are under the directory named by `/tmp/witchery-ridden-save-import-current`. See `chain-package.log`, `chain-gametest.log`, `chain/chain.log`, `chain/restart.log`, and `integrated/verified.log`.

## Custom Potion and Queued Effect Saves (2026-11-23)

## Crystal Ball Camera Recovery (2026-11-23)

- The original camera timed out three seconds after its last heartbeat. The modern client now scopes a view to the current player, client level and target ID. It restores the player camera and input when the target disappears. Forge's player clone event also clears the temporary no-gravity flag if vanilla copied it to the replacement player.
- An isolated Forge 47.4.10 survival client held use on a placed Crystal Ball with a filled Taglock Kit or a bound Waystone. The fixture supplied the arena, altar power and bound items. Removing the cow only from the client reproduced the stale camera and no-gravity flag on the preceding JAR. The new JAR restored the camera and gravity while the server's cow was still alive and the use key remained held.
- The exact matching client and dedicated server JAR was `2704947b67e8032e567ddadaf5c3488a8fbbb0469bd0fa2339ae55e92ba2504e`. The same client recovered after a target crossed to the Nether, after the viewer crossed dimensions, and after death, respawn and reconnect. An orderly dedicated restart during a Waystone view preserved the bound item, closed the old view and let the client open a fresh one. Both client launches reported zero master volume, OpenAL No Output, and no focus activation. The local evidence is under the directory named by `/tmp/witchery-scrying-lifecycle-current`. It contains `baseline-repro-final.log` and a `final-path` file pointing to the matching JAR run's `fixed.log` and `restart.log`.
- Local Java 17 JAR reobfuscation and all 155 required Forge GameTests passed. See `final-package.log` and `final-gametest.log` alongside the baseline log.

## Legacy Poppet Shelf Chunk Tickets (2026-11-23)

- An original Witchery 0.24.1 server placed a shelf at `(-211, 4, -1095)`, stored a tagged Death Protection Poppet in slot 3, and moved spawn 1,235 blocks away. Old Forge wrote `TicketList` as uncompressed NBT in `forcedchunks.dat`. The preceding packaged port left that shelf's chunk unloaded after import.
- The modern import restores the Witchery ticket for surviving shelves in each loaded dimension's original `forcedchunks.dat`. It reuses the same Forge ticking ticket and active shelf registration used for normal placement. Leave the original file in the copied world, then restart normally after import. The fixture and headless server logs are under the directory named by `/tmp/witchery-legacy-shelf-ticket-current`.
- Packaged Forge loaded the original stack and exactly one ticking ticket on first import, after a normal dedicated restart, and when importing again from the preceding port's converted world. Breaking the shelf cleared the modern ticket. An explicit re-import left the removed shelf unforced and preserved the original file byte for byte. See `legacy-shelf-ticket-packaged-complete.log` and `legacy-shelf-ticket-reimport.log` in the directory named by `/tmp/witchery-flowing-spirit-current`.
- Java 17 data generation, JAR reobfuscation, and all 153 checked-in Forge GameTests passed. See `legacy-shelf-ticket-final-data.log`, `legacy-shelf-ticket-verified-jar.log`, and `legacy-shelf-ticket-verified-gametest.log`. ## Auram Werewolf Ranks Six Through Ten (2026-11-23)

- The player completed ten unarmed airborne kills and turned in the rank-five hunt. A grounded control gave no credit, and an eleventh airborne kill left the counter at ten. Counted trials retain `STARTED` at the target count until the player turns them in at the altar. See `auram-wolf-airborne-trial.log` in the directory named by `/tmp/witchery-flowing-spirit-current`.

The Poltergeists threw the result into their enclosure, and the player respawned after entering to collect it. The hopper-menu check counted the reward while it was on the cursor. Closing the menu dropped it, and it was absent at the later retry. The fixture restored the single untagged reward from the recorded item data and verified the actual saved inventory slot. See `auram-death-necromantic-final-activation.log` and `auram-death-stone-saved-inventory.log`.
- The survival player grew Snowbells and saved five earned Icy Needles. The fixture transferred drops using a hopper and checked their saved inventory slots. The Witch's Oven made Breath of the Goddess from Birch Saplings and the Distillery made Whiff of Magic. The player grew an Artichoke, used it with stored Purified Milk and Love in a Sleeping batch, and bottled three servings. The fixture supplied vanilla materials and positioned drops from the player's inventory. See `auram-death-ladder-snowbell-stock.log`, `auram-death-ladder-brewing-reagents-resume.log`, and `auram-death-ladder-sleeping-resume.log`.
- The muted player used one serving to enter a normal Dream, harvested four naturally generated Wispy Cotton plants, and woke with the cotton using an Icy Needle. The fixture positioned the player within reach of the plants. The player used stored Odour of Purity and supplied String to spin Fanciful Thread, then crafted the Witch's Ladder with supplied String and Feathers. See `auram-death-ladder-natural-cotton.log` and `auram-death-spin-and-craft-ladder.log`.

## Brew Bag Throwable Catalog and Network (2026-11-23)

## Concurrent Chunk Palette Reads (2026-11-22)

- Minecraft's lighting worker could read a new palette index before the corresponding entry became visible. `SimpleBitStoragePublicationMixin` publishes the updated storage word with a release write and reads it with acquire semantics. Both single-index writers use that ordering. The storage layout and saved block states stay compatible.
- The original isolated probe repeated three times on the patched code. All 255,000 chunk sections and 6,884,129,970 concurrent reads completed with zero missing entries and zero invalid states. The checked-in regression uses both the section setter and the palette container's direct setter.
- All 115 checked-in Forge GameTests passed. Java 17 data generation and JAR reobfuscation passed. The packaged refmap maps all three storage methods and the backing array to their production names.
- Packaged SHA-256 `45ef1e4b8134525abe53a48853b5d6d68bda10123d5141993ea6a016aa842fe3` passed a clean Forge multiplayer and dedicated restart check. The isolated survival client joined the headless world while hidden and unfocused with master volume zero and OpenAL No Output. Light-emitting blocks were replaced across three chunk columns. The restarted server retained the final blocks, torch, and the chest's three Wood Ash. The macOS monitor recorded no activation for the client PID. `/tmp/witchery-palette-publication-current` identifies the private profiles and logs.
- The 668-case replay completed with 667 passes and no palette exception or chunk-store I/O error. The liquid-brew radius assertion failed in that run and passed during a focused four-case replay. Thaumaturge, Lithostitched, and Curios passed all 118 cases. The packaged Auram smoke test saved its original Oven and Altar and a new light-emitting block and chest before stopping normally. Its first reopen reached the dedicated server but the local RCON test connection timed out before reading the saved blocks.

### Chunk Palette Investigation

- The broad replay on the original-color build was waiting for a forced GameTest chunk after its lighting worker threw `MissingPaletteEntryException` in `LinearPalette.valueFor`. The isolated Forge 47.4.10 probe used one writer and four readers on ordinary Minecraft `LevelChunkSection` instances. It reproduced the same exception in all three samples, with 62,321, 66,342, and 45,761 missing-entry reads per 85,000 sections.
- The exact 1.20.1 bytecode takes a volatile palette-container snapshot and copies resize data before publishing it. Entries added afterward and their ordinary bit-storage indices are written separately with plain accesses. This is consistent with the probe and the earlier light-worker stack. The reproduction source, Gradle invocation, and three recorded samples are under `.repro/chunk-palette-publication` in the current scratch directory. [Mojang palette issue](https://bugs.mojang.com/browse/MC-162080)

## Session Memory (2026-11-19)

- The playable port starts with the Witch's Oven. The original recipes are in `old-1.7.10/com/emoniph/witchery/WitcheryRecipes.java` and the machine behavior is in `blocks/BlockWitchesOven.java`.
- Treat the earlier phase snapshots as history. `runData` now runs against Witchery and Forge by default, writes run logs under `build/run-data`, and generates the packaged datapack under `src/generated/resources`.
- Use `-PwithAuramMods` to add the bundled Auram development mods to a launch. Use `./gradlew jar` for packaging because `build` automatically deploys to the local Auram instance. When generating new resources, run `runData` and `jar` in separate invocations. Gradle can run `processResources` before `runData` in a combined invocation, leaving the new files out of the resulting JAR.
- Use a disposable, headless server for gameplay checks. Launch client tests with zero master volume in a background or isolated test session.
- `./gradlew runGameTestServer` starts a separate test world under `build/run-gametest` on each launch. Sources and fixtures under `src/gameTest` are loaded by that task.
- Replaying archived GameTests through a temporary Gradle init script can leave extra classes under the normal GameTest output when the script is removed. Use separate class and resource output directories for the replay or the subsequent checked-in run, and set the Forge run's `MOD_CLASSES` to the main and selected GameTest directories. Confirm the GameTest discovery count in the server log.
- Oven-related ingredients use stable individual IDs through `WitcheryIngredient`. The manifest records the old damage-to-ID mapping. The original `witchery:ingredient` ID remains registered for unconverted stacks.
- `WitcheryRecipeProvider` generates the oven, clay jar, wood ash, witch log charcoal, attuned stone, and funnel recipes. Sapling smelting follows the legacy default of using the shared sapling tag. Charged attuned stones still depend on restoring the charging rite.
- `WitchesOvenBlockEntity` provides the oven's five slots and persists burn, cook, and inventory state. Headless server checks passed for the seven fume outputs, acacia ash with the jar left intact, restart progress, and known and unknown ingredient metadata fixtures.
- The oven reads legacy ingredient metadata after the base item ID is resolved to `witchery:ingredient`. General conversion of numeric 1.7.10 save IDs still needs its own verified workflow.
- A packaged Forge client with master volume zero and the OpenAL null driver can join the isolated server under Linux Xvfb. Survival crafting, firing jars, oven placement, shift-click input and output, the updated oven model, and wood-variant plank recipes were verified there.
- Rowan, alder, and hawthorn use the existing `wood_type` state and retain the existing four family IDs. Non-rowan inventory stacks carry `BlockStateTag.wood_type`. Growable saplings use modern configured trees, with straight trunks for rowan and narrower and broader fancy trunks for alder and hawthorn.
- The initial source of Witchery saplings is Mutandis. Craft Anointing Paste from artichoke, belladonna, mandrake, and snowbell seeds and use it on a vanilla cauldron. Add three buckets of water, heat it with fire for five seconds, then drop in mandrake root and Exhale of the Horned One in either order. An egg added last starts the 210-tick ritual, which yields six Mutandis. Its water and ritual state survive a restart.
- Ordinary Mutandis plant choices come from the `witchery:mutandis_plants` block tag plus all three Witchery sapling variants. Potted plants use the original pot pool. The General Cauldron Brewing notes track the original ordered brews and migration work.
- The muted survival client showed the restored cauldron basin and water surface. It crafted and used the paste, emptied three water buckets, dropped in the ingredients, picked up the six Mutandis, and used one to mutate oak into alder.
- The client harvested the mutated alder sapling and retained its wood variant. Potted plants run Mutandis before vanilla's pot interaction so they mutate in place. The isolated survival client verified the mutation and item consumption.
- The altar forms when six adjoining blocks make a valid 2-by-3 rectangle. The sixth placed member becomes the core. It scans loaded chunks within 14 blocks for plant and natural block contributions and supplies nearby machines within 16 blocks by default. The recipe requires two rowan logs, four stone bricks, a water bottle, and the birch and oak fumes.
- The altar's original menu shows live power, capacity, and recharge multiplier. Its structure and power storage passed 15 production Forge checks and were verified in the muted multiplayer client. Skull and torch bonuses work.
- The distillery uses the nearest altar in range and spends 0.6 power per tick for 800 ticks. It accepts either order for two ingredients and shows up to four clay jars on its model. A batch preserves its progress through power loss and a restart. A blocked output stops the batch with its inputs intact. Existing idle and burning block and block-entity IDs are supported.
- All nine original `witchery:distilling` recipes are in `data/witchery/recipes/distilling`. The last two accept the old port's Demon Heart ID as well as the canonical heart. Quicklime crafts from Wood Ash. Dropping gypsum into a filled Witch's Cauldron empties it, including when it is cold.
- Ritual Chalk crafts from Wood Ash, gypsum, and Tear of the Goddess. The three colored chalk conversions use Ritual Chalk as the final cauldron ingredient. Golden Chalk uses a mandrake root and a gold nugget. Otherwhere uses nether wart, Tear of the Goddess, and an ender pearl. Infernal uses nether wart and blaze powder. The powered rituals wait for the nearest altar to meet the cost and use the legacy cauldron circle discounts.
- All twelve appearances for each white, purple, and red glyph use the supplied texture assets. Chalk draws on solid surfaces and the glyph disappears if its support is removed. The chalk sound is carried over.
- The Heart Glyph uses the original small and medium rite rings of 16 and 28 glyphs. These rings differ from the 12- and 24-glyph cauldron rings. Right-clicking the heart or pulsing it with redstone starts the matching rite. The first rite consumes an Attuned Stone, glowstone dust, redstone, wood ash, quicklime, and 2,000 altar power. Its staged offerings persist on restart and return when cancelled or when power is insufficient.
- Arthana has gold-tier combat attributes and iron durability. Use it on top of an altar to mount it and double the altar range from 16 to 32 blocks. The placed item uses the original `WITCPlacedItem` tag, persists damage and other item tags, and drops the original stack on removal. It also boosts creature ingredient drops on direct kills. Mob tags define the bats and canines for this purpose.
- The disposable Forge GameTests covered Arthana durability and placement, direct player kills with Looting and the legacy no-drop flag, stacked chalk drawing, the Rite of Charging use interaction, and two Mutandis Extremis tests. Extremis brews from nether wart followed by ordinary Mutandis. It preserves applicable crop ages, toggles grass and mycelium, and can turn submerged dirt and its four neighbors into clay.
- The kettle retains `witchery:kettle` for the block, item and block entity. The Forge fluid handler accepts a bucket of water. Six thrown ingredient stacks form an unordered recipe when heated by fire underneath. A completed 1,000-power batch drains the altar once. One manual glass bottle takes one portion, and automation draws bottles from the kettle's buffer. The last portion empties the kettle and preserves any spare bottles.
- The kettle recognizes legacy `Items`, `Ruined`, `LiquidColor`, and flat water `FluidName` and `Amount` fields after legacy item IDs have been normalized. Partial and finished batches, water, and empty bottles persisted across a restart. All 18 production checks passed. Forge's fire tag also accepts soul fire.
- Kettle recipes live in `data/witchery/recipes/kettle`. Brew of Ink and Brew of the Depths yield three portions with no altar charge. Redstone Soup yields one. Its Drop of Luck is made in the Witch's Cauldron using mandrake root, nether wart, Tear of the Goddess, Refined Evil, and a Mutandis Extremis trigger. The combined base cauldron cost is 4,950 before the circle multiplier. Kettle coven and equipment bonuses and the remaining recipe effects are pending.
- The muted multiplayer client crafted, placed, heated, filled and bottled a kettle using the regular survival controls. It threw the completed Brew of Ink and blinded a nearby husk for less time than the direct target. A supplied Brew of the Depths gave Wither II on land, then removed it while maintaining air underwater. Testing used supplied starting materials. All nine disposable Forge GameTests passed.
- The empty Chalice and Candelabra retain their previous block and item IDs. Redstone Soup crafts a separate `witchery:filled_chalice` item that places the same chalice block with `filled=true`. The first chalice found by the altar adds one to capacity if empty or two if filled. One Candelabra adds two to recharge and emits light level 15. Legacy `WITCFilled` data sets the new block state when the chalice loads. Twelve packaged checks, two more Forge GameTests, and crafting and placement in the muted client passed.
- The Spinning Wheel retains `witchery:spinningwheel` and the `witchery:spinning_wheel` menu. It costs 0.6 altar power per tick for 300 ticks and retains progress when the altar stops supplying it. Hopper fibre goes in the top, modifiers go in any of the three original slots, and output extracts horizontally. The `witchery:spinning` data has all four original recipes.
- Fifteen packaged spinning checks, twelve disposable Forge GameTests, the bundled Auram development smoke test, and the muted survival client's manual wheel craft and Golden Thread batch passed. The installed Prism Auram profile uses Forge 47.4.10 and contains 538 enabled mod JARs. It still needs its own isolated packaged smoke test.
- Dream entry ingredients are available. Mature snowbell has a separate 20% Icy Needle drop. The original milk-bucket and cake recipes make three Purified Milk jars. Drinking a jar has a 50% chance to clear one active effect. The unpowered Brew of Love kettle recipe yields three throwable brews. Its splash resets the breeding cooldown for adult animals and pairs adult villagers without a bed search. The enslaved zombie interaction remains pending. Six packaged checks and the complete 14-test Forge suite passed.
- The original Brew of Sleeping recipe yields three drinks. Drinking it in the Overworld leaves a sleeping body and switches to the player's Dream inventory. Icy Needles and Mutandis carry in. Icy Needles and both cotton drops carry out. Other items stay with their respective inventories. The transition gives the opposite item a one-second cooldown so holding use cannot immediately undo the transition. The previous direct spirit portal shortcut is removed while real manifestation is pending.
- Somnian Cotton generates only in the Dream and spreads near source Flowing Spirit. It drops Disturbed Cotton while a player there is having a nightmare, and Wispy Cotton otherwise. The clean packaged server generated both dimensions, found natural cotton only in the Dream, and verified a Wispy Cotton drop. The Forge suite passed all 15 tests, including a seeded spreading check.
- The muted multiplayer client brewed three Sleeping brews by hand. On a packaged server, the player restarted during a nightmare with both inventories and the sleeping body intact. The Icy Needle returned the player to the body with the original health, damaged Overworld gear and harvested Disturbed Cotton. The next entry recovered the separate Dream inventory. Body destruction and lethal Dream damage both woke the player. Large position changes occasionally logged a vanilla movement warning during the dimension transition.
- The ordinary Nightmare retains `witchery:nightmare`. It spawns near a player with an active nightmare, persists the victim by name and UUID, and drops Mellifluous Hunger in the Dream. The original defense, damage cap, armor swipe, Charm of Disrupted Dreams, model and sounds are present. The 17-test suite passed, and the muted client verified natural spawning, blocked follow-up damage, and returning with the dropped ingredient. Huntsman's Spear is recognized for the defense reduction. Its source and own weapon behavior still depend on the Horned Huntsman port.
- Dreamweaves use horizontal facing and five weave variants on `witchery:dreamcatcher`. The original `WITCWeaveID` restores the variant during ticking. Breaking the support drops the matching item. All five original recipes are available. The muted client crafted and hung the Dreamweave of Nightmares and confirmed its interaction with the Fleet Feet weave when using a bed. With the Nightmare weave, two cotton plants and three Flowing Spirit sources, a Sleeping brew produced a normal dream. The player harvested and took home both cotton variants in separate trips, added three supplied fibre to each batch, and collected both original threads from powered wheels.
- Brew of Flowing Spirit uses the Dream-restricted kettle recipe and carries across waking. The distillery accepts it and Oil of Vitriol with two clay jars. The two throwable fluid brews place the source at impact and apply the original Regen or Weakness effects on contact, subject to vanilla undead effect immunity. Flowing Spirit converts dropped Disturbed Cotton to Wispy Cotton and preserves its stack count. Registered fluids now have client sprite bindings, which resolved the earlier tesselation crash. Fourteen packaged checks passed. The muted client bottled the brew, woke with it, and threw it to make the green pool.
- A two-by-two interior of a snow-block frame ignites in the Dream when a Flowing Spirit source hits its bottom. The portal can use either horizontal axis and collapses if its frame is broken. The 1,000-power Necromantic Stone rite requires night. The 5,000-power Manifestation rite uses the small Otherwhere ring with six offerings and returns the Necromantic Stone. It grants 150 seconds to players in range and refunds offerings if nobody is there. Covens and the stone's undead control are pending.
- Enter an active spirit portal with manifestation time to visit the Overworld as a ghost. The sleeping body remains, the Dream inventory is parked, and only Icy Needles come through. Returning drops ordinary acquired items, restores the Dream inventory, and preserves the remaining timer. Needle return, countdown, restart during manifestation, expiry, and the final wake with an original damaged shield and diamonds all passed in the muted client. Arrival prefers safe footing near the anchor when its immediate column is obstructed.
- Both Demon summons need a 40-glyph Infernal ring and 3,000 altar power. The first route takes a villager from within the ring. The second uses a blaze rod and both Attuned Stones in place of the blaze powder and villager. Obstructing the summon or omitting the required villager returns the offerings. The packaged headless checks covered both rites.
- A Demon trades two Demon Hearts at most. Its generated offers and remaining stock are saved in `Bargains`. Devil's Tongue lowers eligible costs and loses five durability per completed bargain. The Demon takes at most 15 damage per hit and can throw large fireballs. Blaze-rod and magma-cream bargains can make it explode after 50 ticks. Animal trading through the legacy charms remains pending.
- A Demon Heart can be eaten or sneak-placed to pulse and shed light. Breaking the block drops the canonical ingredient. Its original ingredient metadata is 23 and Infernal Blood is 80. The Wicker Bundle's interaction with Infernal Blood is still pending.
- The muted multiplayer client activated the no-villager rite, saw the original Demon model, and bought its stock of two hearts for two magma creams each while holding Devil's Tongue. It placed both hearts and drank a Sleeping brew beside the legacy demonic setup. Both fireball sizes, an ordinary Nightmare and a Demon spawned naturally. The fight pushed the player off the first elevated test platform and the server disconnected them during the fall. With a wider platform, the player stayed connected, woke with an Icy Needle, and the remaining Dream Demons despawned. The three Dream atmosphere variants still need work.
- Bone and flint craft eight Bone Needles. A needle and glass bottle craft one Taglock Kit. Use a kit on a creature or a player's registered bed or coffin, sneak-use at a bed for a self sample, or approach a player directly with the old facing and sneak odds. Collecting from a stack fills one kit. Creature samples use the original UUID halves and name, and player samples preserve the original name with the UUID saved alongside it. The muted client crafted and collected these samples. Leech Chests and Blood Roses supply additional signatures.
- Brew of Grotesque yields three drinks from its 500-power kettle recipe. Drinking one applies the four-minute repulsion and Demon mask. The model uses the player head layer, and the effect migrates remaining legacy `witcheryGrotesque` time on login. The muted client bottled the original brew and verified the effect and rendering.
- Curse of Waking Nightmare uses a medium Infernal ring during thunder and 10,000 power. The cure uses a small white ring and 2,000 power. Both require a filled Taglock Kit and return the staged items when the target cannot be resolved. The rite writes the legacy `witcheryWakingNightmare` value. Waking Nightmares are assigned to their victim, share the original outside-Dream damage and defense, and cannot accumulate around one afflicted target. The Taglock Curses and Cures milestone describes the shared familiar, coven and protection rules.
- The packaged server validated empty and legacy name-only taglocks and refunded the five offerings when a legacy target was offline. The muted client used its own collected taglock to apply the curse, then retained it through death and a server restart. A supplied status effect spawned a Waking Nightmare, which remained after the effect was removed while the rite curse persisted. A second self taglock and the cure ritual removed the curse and Weakness, and the Nightmare despawned. All 24 disposable Forge GameTests passed on a fresh world.
- The Dream uses Forge's dimension effects with the original pink daylight, green nightmare fog, red demonic fog, and a starless sky. The dedicated server sends the highest active nightmare level to Dream clients once per second. Both muted clients showed the shared green fog. One remained a normal dreamer and rejoined during the other's demonic nightmare with red fog. Waking the other player restored pink daylight. Additive blending fixed the dark edges around the sun and lunar phases. One initial Xvfb run ended in a native allocator abort. The relaunched client tested both celestial textures and stayed connected alongside the observer for more than six minutes.
- New Mirror chunks use the original 16-block cell grid from Y 0 through 254. At each level the room interior occupies X and Z offsets 4 through 12 and Y offsets 6 through 14. Their solid, unbreakable walls use the original animated brew textures and tint. Seventeen fresh-world and restart checks passed, as did all three checks on upgrading a disposable save from the previous port. Existing chunks retain their saved contents and new ones use the chamber generator.
- The 2,000-power medium white Rite of the Mirror consumes a living Demon, Brew of Hollow Tears, gold, and glass. The muted client used that rite and mounted its reward as a two-block mirror, then walked into its assigned chamber. The previous port's `mirrorblock` item uses the same placement logic. The source and exit retain the old position and slain fields and share a connection UUID. Players keep only their own chamber entry in `PlayerPersisted.MirrorWorldEntryPoint`. The old right-click shortcuts have been removed.
- The Reflection in the room copies the player's appearance and gear, has 100 health, caps incoming hits at six before armor, and drops one Demon Heart. Its initial spawn waits for the room chunk's entity data to load. This prevented a duplicate reproduced in the muted client. The same UUID survived death, re-entry, and a clean server restart. The client used a real axe hit and collected the Heart after the test fixture shortened the fight. Both ends recorded the defeat. The player returned to the exterior, recovered both halves as one item, and remounted at a new coordinate without losing the linked chamber. A second facing mirror gave the defeated frame an Overworld path. The dedicated-server movement baseline resets on crossing to avoid the original position correction.
- Forge submits the chat event from a background executor, so the Mirror schedules recognized phrases on the main server thread. “Mirror, mirror, send me home” returns to the chamber entrance and has a five-minute cooldown. “Mirror, mirror, I give up” returns to the respawn location with a one-hour cooldown. Both phrases and cooldown messages worked in the muted client. `MirrorEscape2` survived a restart and retained its remaining time. All 26 Forge GameTests passed in a disposable world.
- The muted client walked into a defeated mirror and emerged five blocks higher through a second mirror with the same facing. In the Mirror, its eastern frame blocked entry when the altar had only 1,000 power. With sufficient power it crossed the solid chamber wall and spent 3,000. During a two-client test, the second player mined and carried the exterior mirror to another position. The first player walked through the disconnected room exit and emerged at the carrier. Remounting the mirror restored the original chamber. The second player used the exit phrase to return to the configured respawn point.
- Set `shrinkMirrorWorld = true` in the world's `serverconfig/witchery-server.toml` before starting the game. New Mirror chunks use the legacy Y 0–127 generation span. The modern dimension bounds preserve previously generated chunks and linked rooms. The `witchery_mirror_rooms` save keeps its existing `NextCell` index and skips tiers above the eighth for new links. Switching back resumes full-height generation in new chunks. All 12 packaged geometry checks, all 307 required Forge GameTests, data generation and JAR reobfuscation passed. A packaged server fixture seeded `NextCell` at seven. The muted survival client mounted its first frame and walked in and out of `(516, 120, 8)`. The cursor saved at eight. After restart, the link persisted and the player mounted a second frame that opened `(532, 8, 8)` with the correct return link.
- Right-click either half of an inhabited exterior frame to consult the Mirror Face. The face saves the original `WITCTicksAlive`, faces the viewer and disappears after ten seconds. It uses the original `mirrorface.png` and `mob.reflection.speech` sample. The frame polls for visitors up to four blocks in front of the upper half every 40 ticks. `Favorite`, `Fairest`, `PlayersSeen` and `LastFairestSpawnTime` survive loading and harvesting. A legacy string profile `Id` migrates to the modern UUID array. The server retains conversation history in its save and sends replies through chat.
- The server setting `newFairestOfThemAllSpawnChance` defaults to 0.01 and ranges up to 1.0. A new Fairest needs eligible ground 50–99 blocks away along both horizontal axes. The four variants retain `FollowerType` values 1–4 and use the original names and skins. They persist and drop one Heart of Gold, mapped from legacy ingredient damage 165.
- In a packaged dedicated fixture, a muted survival client saw the Face and two rapid clicks opened one conversation. Another muted client first stood out of range and later asked from the frame and received the bearing to the favorite. The favorite's next answer named the visitor. The fixture supplied suitable terrain and set the spawn chance to 1.0. It produced a type-four Fairest named Thomas Tailor. After a dedicated restart the Mirror directed the player to the same Fairest, the player saw the type-four skin, killed the weakened Fairest, and recovered the Heart of Gold. The next conversation occurred during the saved two-minute cooldown and produced no replacement. All 309 required Forge GameTests, data generation and JAR reobfuscation passed. The packaged SHA-256 is `09febf99f1d02d20263301701570d190c83518e5dc8cedd9dc7b8ee6b95e2730`.
- Right-click an inhabited exterior Mirror with a player-bound Taglock Kit for 4,000 altar power. Disguise uses creature form 4. The capability accepts the original `WitcheryExtendedPlayer.CreatureType` and `LastPlayerSkin` and saves the modern `CreatureTypeOrdinal` and `OtherPlayerSkin`. The Taglock remains filled. An empty or self-bound kit clears the disguise for free. The player renderer uses the named player's skin and model through the vanilla skin manager. Offline names use the profile cache or an asynchronous Mojang profile lookup.
- `allowNameplateMasquerading` and `allowChatMasquerading` default to `true`. Ordinary chat changes its presentation while retaining its sender for vanilla chat, filtering, and signed identity. The displayed name suggests a message to the original player. Creative server operators in the same dimension get the reveal. In the packaged muted test, a survival player collected the second player's Taglock and used it at the Mirror. The observer saw the matching skin and label and received the operator message. After a dedicated restart with both settings disabled, the second client saw the real name in chat and on the restored disguise. An unbound kit restored the original skin with no altar charge. The two test profiles were local, so the external offline account lookup still needs verification.
- A Quartz Sphere draws 2,000 power and becomes `witchery:dupgrenade` with the original `Owner` item tag. A stack loses one sphere while a single sphere becomes the grenade in place. The shared `witchery:grenade` projectile accepts legacy `Mode:1` and a string `Owner`. Current saves keep the thrower's UUID through vanilla projectile data and store the copy's name in the carried item. A duplicate returns one sphere on impact and spawns `witchery:follower` with `FollowerType:5`, `Skin`, and `TicksToLive` set to ten seconds. For a present owner it copies the skin and equipment and draws nearby hostile mobs already pursuing that owner. Copied equipment has zero drop chance.
- The two muted clients saw the copied armor and torch, and the duplicate took skeleton arrow damage before expiring. The thrower recovered a single sphere. The redstone-powered dispenser emptied its slot, emitted another named duplicate and left another sphere. A focused GameTest covers impact on the final fuse tick, legacy load, short life and suppression of the sun grenade's effects. The initial full run flagged an existing werevillager fixture that queried all villagers in its test area. Restricting it to the converted creature's original identity produced a clean fresh run of all 311 required tests. Data generation and JAR reobfuscation passed. The packaged SHA-256 is `594f7f433e4231b302740cfa878b451589b377a2a4c27c6024741a87b1a2675a`.
- Revealing has an unpowered Kettle version and an Odour of Purity cauldron action. The fixed brew keeps subtype 124 and the original sprite. A regular Night Vision potion matches the Kettle recipe. Named legacy potion damage 8198 imports into that potion. The original splash radius is five blocks, or eight with Toad Brew Mastery. The cauldron action costs 100 base power and four effect levels and runs in ingredient order. Both versions clear invisibility, Mirror disguise form 4, and the saved Spectre `Obscured` state. Two muted clients verified that one of three Kettle portions revealed the disguised player and both spectral creatures. An out-of-range pig kept its invisibility.
- Heart of Gold subtype 165 uses four effect levels and 500 base power. Attraction and its fermented-eye inversion use the original `(strength + 1) × 16` radius. The recipe updates non-familiar pet ownership and moves ordinary animals. Modern cats use the ownership path that legacy tameable ocelots used. Repulsion uses the modern heightmap and loaded, collision-free footing for its fallback movement. The fixture supplied two prepared cauldrons and their dropped ingredients. The client bottled and drank the 650-power attraction and 675-power repulsion brews. The wild wolf was tamed, another player's wolf was released, a cow moved toward and away from the drinker, and the bonded Toad retained its owner and `Familiar` flag. The other pet beyond the effect radius stayed wild.
- The restart fixture retained two Kettle servings, the wolves' changed ownership, the bonded Toad, and the revealed Spectre's state. A bottled 250-power Odour of Purity brew kept its ordered ingredients on the disguised survival player. Drinking it after the restart cleared the disguise and invisibility and returned an empty bottle. Four new GameTests cover both spider eyes, legacy potion and brew tags, vertical splash coverage, ordered invisibility and revealing, and pet ownership. All 315 required Forge GameTests, data generation and JAR reobfuscation passed. The packaged SHA-256 is `77b97c73a78a97599ba2d994c2e3e8fd3835721df6593759359c6295a23aeceb`.
- Reflection copies the owner's armor, active effects, vampire status, disguise, and strongest additive-damage hotbar weapon. Wolfman form clears its weapon and gives six base damage. Its handbow copies enchantments and chooses bolts for the opponent. Mystic Branch casts the original weighted spells, with Attraho also available while idle with line of sight. Legacy `Model` and `Vampire` preserve those copied traits on load.
- Rite 96 uses the large Infernal circle, Mirror, Ender Dew, Blaze Powder, and Quartz Sphere and costs 5,000 altar power. Obstructions return all four items after charging power. The summons saves `FreeSpawn` and has no lifetime limit. Its death leaves the Mirror chamber's defeat record alone. Protocol 61 synchronizes the rite and appearance.
- Java 17 data generation and reobfuscation passed. All 318 required Forge GameTests passed, including reloading the active rite, the previous port's Mirror item, obstruction refunds, Silver and Holy Bolts with Flame, and the legacy Wolfman and Vampire flags. The packaged dedicated restart resumed a rite with one offering consumed. A muted Xvfb survival client confirmed the summoned Reflection's Silver Bolts, Attraho, Ignianima and Expelliarmus, and its Wolfman model. The tested JAR SHA-256 is `cf0e3d10c02143dfe20c8ad1f597df673ef7cea945c7925208461456507e57f6`.
- The Torment generator builds six independently seeded 31-by-31-cell mazes with floors at Y 10, 25, 40, 55, 70, and 85. The entry is near (8,8) and the exit near (8,68). Mazes have original force walls and roofs, mycelium-textured Torment Stone, rare vanilla mycelium, and three cache rooms each. In 100 seed fixtures all 600 floors had routes from the entry to the exit and all three caches. The fixed modern seed replaces the legacy population order so neighboring chunks agree.
- The registered Refilling Chest uses the normal chest screen, renderer and inventory. It persists its original `WITCLifeTicks` counter, fills immediately in Torment, and refills every 72,000 loaded ticks when empty. The modern dungeon loot table supplies two to five stacks. All 59 packaged headless checks passed, including real chest loot, an empty-cache refill, skipping a still-stocked cache, and save persistence. Portal physics and the boss progression are pending.
- The muted survival client opened an actual generated Torment chest, saw its lid and inventory render, and collected bread from it. The upgrade fixture preserved its old diamond marker and found persisted Nether terrain at chunk (0,4), which would otherwise contain the new maze exits. The next travel cycle must choose a fresh maze footprint when upgrading that terrain.
- Torment Portal retains its block ID and adds a horizontal axis, full collision routing, light, flame particles, and the original red tint on the animated vanilla portal sprite. `TormentTravel` writes the legacy `WITCForceTorment` request and processes it on the server tick. Entry chooses among the six real maze starts. Exits ordinarily use the Overworld respawn location. The five-percent branch sends the player to a maze entrance again. The muted client entered every floor and made 32 ordinary exits. A seeded GameTest verified the rare request and that the two portal blocks cannot reroll one collision. The 27 required tests passed. The Dream loot fixture waits for its entity chunk to load before checking for the existing drop.
- Upgraded Torment saves retain their original Nether-like chunks. Travel checks the original maze footprint and saves an alternate X offset of 1024 if any of its chunks predate the generator. The muted client saw the animated red portal, arrived at X 1032.5 in the alternate maze, and walked through the generated exit to its configured respawn point. A flushed save retained the offset. After restarting, the same portal reached another floor in the alternate layout and the old diamond marker remained.
- The Lord of Torment uses its original four-arm winged model and lives at `witchery:lordoftorment`. It has 500 health, caps ordinary hits at five and demonic hits at eight, and ignores fire. The original laugh and hurt samples are present. The archive references a missing death sample, so the port uses the hurt sample at a lower pitch. Its volley includes one Ignianima pulse and three or nine Soulfire projectiles. Soulfire uses the original entity ID. The old scaffold's projectile spawn egg ID remains readable.
- When an Overworld Lord reaches half health, it sends nearby survival players and recorded attackers to the same Torment floor. Saved pending encounters wait for the maze chamber's entities to load and spawn one Lord at 250 health. Two muted survival clients verified this transition on the upgraded maze and saw one Lord. The test supplied the initial boss and shortened the fight around the threshold. Real attacks verified a five-point cap, an actual ranged volley, and the threshold trigger. All 29 Forge GameTests passed, including separate checks for both damage caps, fire immunity, the full volley and the four loot drops.
- A dedicated restart retained both players in the same maze and the same single Lord UUID at 250 health. After shortening the final fight in the fixture, the player killed it with an axe and picked up two enchanted books, a Demon Heart and the Brew of Soul Torment. Drinking the brew consumed it and saved `PlayerPersisted.WITCSpellBook.tormentum`. The player returned to their Overworld respawn through the generated exit. The coven, Imp, contract and actual spellcasting remain pending.
- The medium white Rite of Binding a Familiar costs 8,000 power and uses Tear of the Goddess, Odour of Purity, Whiff of Magic, a diamond, and Infernal Blood. It converts the player's tamed vanilla cat within seven blocks into `witchery:cat` and preserves its name and owner. The original cat variant is the modern black coat. The muted player tamed Bramble with cod and bound it with the actual rite, which had previously refunded all five items and power without a pet. The bound cat had 50 health, took one point of the owner's ten-point injury, and healed with cod. The original five-point armor bonus, fire immunity and offline owner protection passed Forge GameTests.
- The medium white Rite of Calling a Familiar costs 1,000 power and uses Breath of the Goddess, Hint of Rebirth, and Whiff of Magic. The muted client called back Bramble from a loaded Torment chunk with the same UUID. The same client dismissed the seated cat by sneaking and interacting, then called a replacement with the same name, 50 health, and a new UUID. The two rites extend the recipe actions on protocol version 7. All 31 required Forge GameTests passed at this stage.
- A dedicated restart retained the same cat UUID, 50 health, and matching owner record. A fatal hit in the disposable fixture dismissed the familiar, cleared `FamiliarSummoned`, and killed its online owner. The record kept the name for a later call.
- The stable `witchery:covenwitch` registry ID now has a neutral 30-health witch with five legacy skins, original voice, forest and swamp spawns, and the seven original quests. An active familiar is required to receive an offer. Accepted quests preserve their player name, recipient UUID and remaining count. The two 100-health quest pets save trophies keyed to the originating witch's UUID. Six members fit in `PlayerPersisted.WITCCoven`, using the original `WitchName`, `Skin` and `Quest` fields. The entity's synchronized skin advances network compatibility to protocol 8. All 34 required GameTests passed, including actual recipe matching, biome registration, a legacy name-only bones quest, and the saved zombie trophy.
- Quartz Sphere uses quartz, quartz blocks and glass at a crafting table. Happenstance Oil uses Redstone Soup, extended Night Vision, an Eye of Ender, a Golden Carrot, a Spider Eye and Mandrake Root in a 2,000-power kettle batch. The night-only, medium white Rite of the Crystal Ball uses Quartz Sphere, Gold Ingot, Happenstance Oil and 2,000 power. The original Imp rite requires activation with the Seer Stone and at least one coven member. A matching signed Contract of Ownership and 25 experience levels bind the summoned Imp before it can exchange gifts. Its final reward, the Contract of Torment, consumes personal infusion charge.
- The muted survival client crafted a Quartz Sphere at the crafting table, bottled Happenstance Oil from a kettle, and dropped its own ingredients into the medium white circle before activating it at night. The Crystal Ball was the request Angelique Redfern gave after the player recalled Bramble. The player collected and offered the ball, and a dedicated restart retained the membership with skin 4 and quest ID 3. The used ball stayed consumed and the recruited witch stayed dismissed.
- The small white Seer Stone rite costs 2,000 power at night and uses Quartz Sphere, Obsidian and Happenstance Oil. The muted client collected the stone, read its familiar Bramble, one coven member and Tormentum knowledge, and held use to recall Angelique for one minute. The separate Coven Witch Combat and Visits milestone verifies the original circle transition from a visitor to a fresh ritual summon.
- A Coven Witch saves defensive drinking as `DrinkTicks` with the held vanilla potion. Drinking stops ranged attacks and subtracts 0.25 from movement speed until the potion finishes. Splash effects use the duration decoded from the original potion metadata. The Seer Stone dismisses nearby members at circle activation, then seats the original roster in one-second steps. Temporary witches keep their saved `Sitting` and `SuicideIn` fields. An older port's NoAI ritual snapshot resumes as a seated witch with AI.
- `allowCovenWitchVisits` defaults to true. Within sixteen blocks of the owner's same-dimension respawn point, the once-in-twenty five-second check moves a nearby visitor or creates a ninety-second visitor from the roster. Expiry and ritual dismissal preserve the roster. Death removes the matching member when the owner is present. A packaged import of an original named witch preserved both UUIDs, her skin, quest and remaining lifetime through an ordinary restart.
- Coven Witch and Hobgoblin shelter uses occupied 1.20.1 village points of interest in place of the legacy `VillageDoorInfo` list. Inspect five positions on each side of loaded wooden doors with the motion-blocking heightmap, then use ground navigation to reach a covered tile. The hut itself can be bedless. The original night, rain and dry-biome checks remain, and a leashed Hobgoblin yields to its lead. Daytime door navigation is restricted near the covered side of a door until night or departure. The shelter fixtures build above normal terrain because Forge places the structure markers below the surface. On packaged Forge, a generated hut's resident opened and closed its door in a clear night and daytime rain. A test Hobgoblin did the same at noon after a local desert biome change.
- Finishing the Crystal Ball rite sets `PlayerPersisted.WITCFTeller`. A placed ball charges 500 power from an altar in range and saves its `LastUsedTime`. The closest eligible player in the reading area, or the reader when alone, receives one saved `WITCPredict.WITCPreList` entry with legacy ID and wall-clock tick. The muted survival client made the ball through its recipe chain, saw its animated model and undead reading, and verified the cooldown and repeated reading. Due Nether forecasts use the standard modern dimension transfer and can spawn a Blaze. All 101 required GameTests passed at this stage.
- A dedicated restart retained the player's fortune-teller training and undead forecast with the same timestamp. The placed ball retained `LastUsedTime`. A live zombie hit the reconnected survival player and cleared the forecast.
- Mining forecasts 5–8 and 17 run through Forge's block loot context. Iron and Coal recognize their Deepslate ores, and Deepslate also counts as base stone. Their legacy bonus amounts and probability thresholds are preserved. Treasure uses the modern abandoned mineshaft loot table and requires solid surroundings without overwriting a container. The muted survival client kept a seeded Coal forecast through a loot preview, then mined Deepslate and picked up 16 Coal. A separate saved Treasure forecast exposed a chest while digging dirt. The player opened it and took a Golden Apple. All 103 required GameTests, data generation and JAR reobfuscation passed at this stage.
- Love and rescues use original IDs 9, 13 and 14. The farmer follows the player's UUID for up to 1,000 ticks through vanilla Villager brain memories. The muted player stood still while the farmer approached and a baby villager appeared. Both rescue forecasts survived load in old-format player fixtures. Actual zombie strikes summoned a 300-tick Owl and a wild Wolf. The Owl dealt 11.52 damage to its attacker during the observed window, and the Wolf dealt 31.49 to its own. All 105 required GameTests passed. The legacy sound mapping for the Love encounter references a sample missing from the archive.
- A trained player read for a second muted survival client on dedicated Forge. The second player saw the Wet message and retained its forecast until stepping into water. The reader's record stayed empty.
- Baba Yaga uses the original `witchery:babayaga` ID, 500 health, four armor, 15-point incoming damage cap, wooden sword counter, potion combat and owner-based visits. The original model, texture, voice and boss HUD are active. Both forecasts triggered from aged legacy records in a packaged server with two muted survival clients. The hostile encounter applied Poison. The ally kept the second player's name and eventually departed. A nearby name-only legacy ally fixture supplied Spectral Dust, Dog Tongue and Bat Wool to its owner, and the client saw the mortar and pestle. A separate fight fixture showed no reflected damage from a wooden sword and reflected damage from a stone sword. Lowering the fixture's health shortened the fight before the final wooden sword hit. The boss dropped Baba's Hat, an enchanted book and ingredients. All 107 required GameTests, data generation and JAR reobfuscation passed at this stage.
- Holding use on the Crystal Ball with a location-bound Waystone draws 500 altar power and opens the transient `witchery:eye` within 150 blocks in the same dimension. The Eye rises half a block per tick for 200 ticks and protocol 21 streams terrain and entities from its position. A temporary ticket keeps the physical player active at the ball. Both a clean muted Forge client and an isolated profile with Auram's Embeddium 0.3.31 and Oculus 1.8.0 displayed the same 126-block destination and returned to the ball. The renderer profile used its default shader setting. A second client saw the player's body beside the ball. The clean client held through Eye expiry and got its normal view without spawning another Eye. Actual distant and Nether stones were refused. Cross-dimension travel for nonplayer entities now keeps the arrival chunk active long enough to load its entity storage. All 108 required GameTests, data generation and JAR reobfuscation passed.
- Protocol 22 extends the Crystal Ball camera to a living subject. A filled Taglock Kit resolves the original player name or creature UUID halves and accepts the modern player UUID. A loaded subject in the same dimension can be viewed beyond the Waystone's distance cap. The ball refuses an empty kit before charging power. A filled kit for an unavailable subject draws the original 500 power. A self sample opens the rising Eye. The muted client collected bed and cow samples, followed the second player to a new chunk, viewed the cow, and regained its own view when the cow left the dimension. Restarting with a view active preserved the kit, physical player position, and cow UUID. All 109 required GameTests passed.
- Legacy ingredient subtype 72 is Impregnated Leather. Its shaped recipe yields four pieces. The Witch's Hat, Witch's Robes and Necromancer's Robes use the original patterns, dye, armor geometry and sprites. Shapeless crafting fully repairs either hat with three Impregnated Leather or either robe with four. A muted survival client crafted all three recipes and repaired a supplied damaged robe with an existing dye, name and Protection enchantment. The dyes tint the item sprite and the worn model. Legacy clothing in migrated inventories retains root `Damage` and `display.color`.
- Manual Kettle bottling uses the original independent rolls. The Witch's Hat contributes 35 percent, Baba's Hat contributes 25 percent to each of two rolls, a matching robe adds 35 percent to the primary roll, and an active Toad adds five percent to eligible rolls. The Witch's Robes also deter Creepers and the Necromancer's Robes deter mobs with the undead type. Baba's Hat can evade direct entity damage or explosions for five infusion charge outside Torment. The muted client verified the ward, both hat models and the Necromancer's shoulder pads. The ward's wolf-form and draining-bolt exceptions depend on those features.
- Brew of Raising preserves legacy subtype 65, the 500-power recipe, its original 1–3 mob roll and the 60/37/3-percent Zombie, Skeleton and Zombified Piglin mix. Its projectile raises undead on a top hit and drops a recoverable bottle on other hits. Raising checks modern world bounds, protected blocks and Forge break cancellation before clearing space. Raised undead share the Infernal binding with `WITCEnslaverName` and the owner's UUID. The Necromantic Stone issues attack or move orders without drawing infusion charge. The muted client raised two zombies and sent them against a husk. All 113 required GameTests, data generation and JAR reobfuscation passed.
- After a dedicated restart the two raised zombies retained their UUIDs and both owner fields. The rejoined client retained three Brews of Raising, the repaired named robe, Baba's Hat and 158 remaining infusion charge. The client used the same reobfuscated JAR as the dedicated server, master volume zero and OpenAL's No Output device.
- The original Kettle recipes and ingredient subtypes 57, 58, 59 and 61 supply Vines, Webs, Thorns and Sprouting. The Web ingredient crafts from cobweb and four string. Webs place the original cross, with diagonal strands for an active Toad. Thorns place cactus and convert suitable ground to sand subject to ordinary support and block protection. Vines grow along sturdy wall faces and Sprouting preserves the source species and impact axis for legacy wood, adding nearby leaves along the branch. The Sprouting recipe requires the three distinct saved Witchery sapling variants. Baba Yaga throws Webs and Thorns at their original attack indices and adds Vines and Sprouting to her visit gifts and combat loot.
- Ink and Love use the Toad's original five-block splash. Love pairs living adult zombies owned by the thrower. Both adults become Zombie Villagers and one wild baby appears. Forge's modern entity conversion gives the parents new entity UUIDs and transfers their ownership data, health, equipment, and active effects. The muted client threw Love near the two zombies it previously raised. Both converted with owner data and the baby appeared. After a packaged restart the parents remained owned, the terrain stayed changed, and two Webs servings stayed in the Kettle. All 118 required GameTests passed, including Baba's two projectile choices, blocked branch growth, the diagonal Toad webs, and mixed owned and wild zombies.
- Baba now selects all seven legacy special brews at their original 12 indices, with seven possible allied gifts and ten possible death offerings. Frog's Tongue moves creatures toward the thrower, Cursed Leaping adds height with Jump Boost IV, and Hitchcock sends three or four temporary owls at a direct target for up to 400 ticks. Bats summons ten or fourteen marked bats and applies Slowness VI. Wasting applies Poison, drains food and applies Hunger to players, applies Wither to other creatures, and removes vulnerable plants through the modern terrain protection checks. The muted client exercised all five effects and crafted Bat Ball. The GameTests verified both motion vectors, enhanced bats, crops, and every Baba attack choice.
- Kettle recipes support `bottling_familiar` under network protocol 23. The finished batch saves its recipe ID and legacy batches fall back to their saved result. The three original gates are Cat for Cursed Leaping, Toad for Frog's Tongue, and Owl for Hitchcock. The dedicated server kept the three Hitchcock portions and the glass bottle intact through attempted extraction and restart. The player called their Owl with the actual calling rite and bottled the stored brew. All 123 required GameTests, data generation and JAR reobfuscation passed.
- The Mutating Sprig converts a cactus surrounded by four Spanish Moss blocks above water and four loaded Grasspers into Wild Bramble. Two Grasspers carry Bone Meal and two carry Blaze Powder. Sugar Cane uses the same arrangement with four Ender Pearls and makes Ender Bramble. Mature Wheat uses four Somnian Cotton plants and four diagonal water sources to make Wormwood. Both Brambles share the legacy `witchery:bramble` ID. The 1.20.1 default retains the existing Wild appearance, and explicit legacy `Damage` values 0 and 1 import Ender and Wild respectively. The item appearance and loot carry the `wild` state.
- The muted survival client made all three mutations and mined Wild Bramble. It tossed the harvested item into the heated Kettle, producing two Infused Brew Base servings for 3,000 altar power. The second recipe uses an existing Base, Flowing Spirit, Hint of Rebirth, Mandrake Root, Bat Wool and Wild Bramble. The completed batch saves the second recipe ID so it retains its original zero bottling bonus.
- The Grave rite uses small and medium Ritual Chalk rings, 3,000 power, an Infused Brew Base, bone, Web, a Necromantic Stone and a living zombie. It returns the stone and creates legacy subtype 123. The drink stores the original `WITCInfusedBrewType` and `WITCInfusedBrewStart` for a two-hour duration. Sneaking with the stone binds undead at the target up to 15 blocks away and updates `WITCBrewGraveTime` for the ten-second cooldown. The original Soaring and Grave icons show rounded-up remaining minutes from the synchronized duration. The muted client brewed and drank the Grave, bound a zombie, failed an immediate second use and bound a skeleton when the cooldown finished. It kept the same infusion charge throughout.
- Ender Bramble schedules travel for the next server tick to let the movement packet finish first. The muted client stepped into it and moved to safe terrain roughly 370 blocks from the test platform. On a dedicated restart, the Grave skull showed 117 minutes remaining, the zombie kept its UUID and owner, and the start time, cooldown timestamp, and infusion charge were unchanged. All 129 required Forge GameTests, data generation, and the reobfuscated build passed.

### Plant Mines and Glyphic Transformation (2026-11-20)

- Plant Mines retain `witchery:plantmine`. The twelve recipes use poppy, dandelion, or dead bush with two stone pressure plates and three fixed brews. Placed traps store `variant`. Items store `BlockStateTag.variant` and accept legacy damage values 0–11 through the inventory conversion helper. General mining and support loss drop nothing. The Boline returns the exact variant and takes one wear.
- The four effects reuse the thrown brew terrain and blindness logic. Webs form a cross, Ink applies up to 400 Blindness ticks, Thorns grows a four-block cactus on sand, and Sprouting grows ten logs and lifts creatures above the branch.
- Rite 67 consumes Gypsum, Arthana, and 1,000 power. Fresh dropped chalk selects the ring size and target color. The first eligible chalk determines the color and only its color counts toward the ring size. One stick is consumed. Missing or damaged chalk returns the two offerings after charging power. Protocol 62 adds this action.
- In a fresh disposable world, the muted survival client crafted four Dandelions of Webs with supplied ingredients, planted one and recovered it with the Boline, then triggered a second by walking into it. It tossed Gypsum, Arthana, and two Otherwhere Chalk sticks onto a prepared medium ring and started the rite. The dedicated restart after one offering was consumed recolored all 28 glyphs and preserved their patterns. The out-of-ring white glyph, one chalk, and a separately planted trap remained.
- Java 17 data generation, JAR reobfuscation, and all 323 required GameTests passed. New checks cover all twelve recipes and legacy stack variants, recovery, all four contact effects, a large ring reload, and the refund with worn chalk. Packaged SHA-256 is `c4ef3cdfcfbf52ad9d0054b0ae55c63e20c34ac39fa5605e85f1c51d49e40611`.

### Forestation, Volcanoes and Wicker Bundles (2026-11-20)

- Nine saplings craft one plain Wicker Bundle. Five plain bundles and Infernal Blood yield five Bloodied Wicker Bundles. The shared ID remains `witchery:wickerbundle`, placed variants keep `bundle_type`, and item metadata 1 imports as `BlockStateTag.bundle_type=bloodied`.
- Rites 70–76 use a medium white ring, oak, spruce, birch, jungle, rowan, alder, or hawthorn sapling, plain Wicker Bundle, Brew of Sprouting, an Ent Branch, and 4,000 power. They sample the original radius of 20 blocks and up to seven levels above and below their target, applying up to eleven bonemeal attempts with modern sapling placement and configured tree features. Every coven member adds two blocks of radius and five pulses.

It checks for lava below the target, grows an eight-block cone, feeds lava up to a permanent summit source, and opens the shaft. Coven members add two to the radius and four to the height. Modern build limits, block entities, and Forge protection events bound the terrain changes.
- Both rites accept a dropped or Grassper-held, location-bound Waystone in the current dimension. A valid target can be 50 blocks away, plus 50 per coven member. A remote cast leaves its origin Heart Glyph intact. Missing lava and targets beyond reach refund the offerings after charging power.

Legacy terrain rite IDs 70–77 read the old `Rituals`, `RitualStages`, `RitualCovens` and `Locations` fields. Active ore rite migration remains supported.
